its language,<br>Pashto, an Iranian tongue, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-392">354</a>; its<br>distribution, character, dialects, literature, and the number of its speakers,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-392">354</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-393">355</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Age, statistics of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-516">478</a>; higher in<br>Aryan N. India than in Mongolian and<br>Dravidian Bengal and Bombay, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-516">478</a>;<br>influenced by ratio of death and birth<br>rates, by famines, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-516">478</a>; a table of age-distribution of the population in India<br>and England, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-552">514</a>; mortality and expectation of life at different ages in<br>India and England, with a table, 514-<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-554">516</a>; age as a factor in mortality from<br>enteric, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-566">528</a>. <em>See also</em> Infantile Mortality, Census Table VII.</p><p class="index-entry">Aghoris, a Saiva sect, eaters of filth and<br>corpses, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-459">421</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Agni, the Vedic god of fire, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-441">403</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Ahmadiyas, the, a recent sect of Islām<br>in the Punjab, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-476">438</a>; their leader,<br>Mullā Ghulām Ahmad, claims to be<br>at once Mahdi and Messiah, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-476">438</a>; the<br>Korān contains all knowledge, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-476">438</a>; discourages religious war, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-476">438</a>; denounces<br>Christianity, Hinduism, the Shiah<br>doctrines, and English education, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-476">438</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Akbar the Great, his tolerance of Hinduism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-472">434</a>; of Christianity, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-480">442</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Alīgarh, the Anglo-Oriental College at,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-476">438</a>; representative of the progressive<br>party in Islām, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-476">438</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Allahābād, meteorology of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-164">126</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Alluvial remains: <em>Elephas namadicus</em>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-138">100</a>; botany of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-241">203</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-242">204</a>; their language Andamanese, really<br>a group of agglutinating languages,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-427">389</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Animism, what is meant by the term,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-468">430</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-469">431</a>, 473 (footnote); a mass of primitive beliefs, the real faith of the people,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-468">430</a>; largely non-Aryan in its origin<br>and distribution, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-470">432</a>; prevalent at all<br>times and everywhere, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-470">432</a>; census<br>returns of only nominal value, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-470">432</a>; in<br>its purest form among the forest races of<br>the Peninsula and Himālayas, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-469">431</a>; their<br>kingdoms and dynasties, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-469">431</a>; its effect<br>on Islām, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-473">435</a>; its fusion with Islām in<br>the Pachpiriyas, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-473">435</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-474">436</a>; the religion<br>of 3 per cent. of the population, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-509">471</a>;<br>the regional distribution of Animists,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-510">472</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-511">473</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Antelopes, three genera peculiar to the<br>Peninsula, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-272">234</a>; the Nīlgai, the Four-horned Antelope, the Indian Antelope<br>or ‘black buck,’ their distribution,<br>haunts and habits, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-273">235</a>; the Tibetan<br>Antelope, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-273">235</a>; Gazelles, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-273">235</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Anthropometry, as applied to Ethnology,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-322">284</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-323">285</a>; conditions favourable to anthropometry and its peculiar value in India,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-323">285</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-324">286</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-326">288</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Apabhramsa (‘corrupt’), title given to<br>the true vernaculars after the Prākrits<br>became literary and polished, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-399">361</a>;<br>the parents of the modern vernaculars,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-400">362</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Approaches by land and gateways of<br>India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-43">5</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/01-frontmatter/#page-6">6</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-48">10</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-56">18</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Arāvalli mountains, their antiquity and<br>degradation, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-39">1</a>; divide Indian desert or<br>the Thar from South Rājputāna, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-71">33</a>;<br>meteorology of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-161">123</a>; migration of Aryans by Jaxartes and Oxus to Khokand and Badakshān, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-391">353</a>, their division into Indo-Aryans, marching south over the Hindu Kush by Kābul to India, and Eranians (Iranians) towards Merv, Persia, Baluchistān, and Afghānistān, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-391">353</a>; Flora of British India<br>varied and rich, but no Natural Order<br>peculiar to it, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-195">157</a>; hence no Indian<br>Flora proper, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-196">158</a>; phytogeographic<br>anomalies few, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-197">159</a>; rhododendron<br>belt of high Eastern Himālayas an exception to general absence of plants<br>giving, like British heaths, a character<br>to wide landscapes, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-197">159</a>; indigenous<br>palms, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-198">160</a>; bamboos, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-198">160</a>; gregarious<br>shrubs, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-198">160</a>; fresh-water flowering<br>plants, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-199">161</a>; number of recorded Natural Orders and species, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-199">161</a>; dominant Orders, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-200">162</a>; proportion of Monocotyledons to Dicotyledons, of genera<br>to species, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-200">162</a>; number of recorded<br>species of Palms, Bamboos, Conifers,<br><em>Cycadeae</em>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-200">162</a>; characteristics of three<br>primary botanical Areas in British India,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-200">162</a>; basis of subdivision into nine<br>botanical Regions, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-200">162</a>; enumeration<br>and limitation of Regions, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-201">163</a>; strict<br>geographical limitations difficult or<br>impossible, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-202">164</a>; rough coincidence of<br>Regions with wet and dry areas of<br>Prain's <em>Plants of Bengal</em>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-202">164</a>; with<br>sub-areas of C. B. Clarke, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-203">165</a>; correspondence of Regions with sixty-four<br>Provinces of <em>Flora Indica</em>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-203">165</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-204">166</a>;<br>details of nine Regions, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-204">166</a>–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-245">207</a>; British Baluchistān,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-247">209</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-248">210</a>. Bibliography, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-249">211</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-250">212</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Brahmā, the first person of the Hindu<br>Triad, in the Epic period, the Creator,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-442">404</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-458">420</a>; now not widely worshipped,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-458">420</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Brāhmanas, the, sacred Sanskrit writings<br>explanatory of the sacrifices and duties<br>of the priests, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-442">404</a>; later in thought<br>than the Vedic Hymns, earlier than the<br>Upanishads, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-444">406</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Brāhmanism, its birth-place in the 'Midland,' 404; a ritualistic and philosophical development of Vedism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-442">404</a>;<br>the Brāhmanas, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-442">404</a>; supremacy of<br>the priestly class, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-442">404</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-443">405</a>; the subjection of the other classes, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-445">407</a>; a<br>system of ritual and worship rather than<br>of religion, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-443">405</a>; its vague eschatology,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-443">405</a>; its human sacrifice, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-443">405</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-444">406</a>;<br>its exclusion of all but Brāhmans from<br>the ascetic fraternities, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-446">408</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-452">414</a>; reaction against, in Buddhism and<br>Jainism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-444">406</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-445">407</a>; the compatibility<br>of Hinduism with both, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-446">408</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-453">415</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-454">416</a>;<br>the evolution from Brāhmanism of modern Hinduism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-450">412</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-455">417</a>. <em>See also</em><br>Caste, Hinduism.</p><p class="index-entry">Brāhmans, ethnology of, the Marāthā<br>Brāhmans, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-331">293</a>; of Hindustān, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-332">294</a>;<br>of Bihār, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-332">294</a>; of Bengal, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-332">294</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-333">295</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-342">304</a>; Nambūdri or Nampūtiri of<br>Malabar, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-357">319</a>; their traditional origin<br>and exceptional customs and ritual, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-357">319</a>;<br>Rārhi of Bengal, legend of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-342">304</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-357">319</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-358">320</a>; Barna, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-364">326</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-369">331</a>; Vyāsokta, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-364">326</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Brāhmans, the, their mythical origin, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-370">332</a>;<br>suppression of the Kshattriyas, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-445">407</a>; first<br>of all castes in social precedence, 324-<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-364">326</a>; their social and ritual relations to<br>other castes, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-362">324</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-364">326</a>; Brāhmans and<br>Brāhmans, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-364">326</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-365">327</a>; their wide diffusion and large number, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-369">331</a>; probably<br>of mixed descent, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-369">331</a>; different theories<br>of the origin of the caste or Brāhmanical system, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-370">332</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-385">347</a>; more orthodox<br>and powerful in South than in North<br>India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-460">422</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Brahmaputra, the river, its origin in Tibet<br>as Tsan-po, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-57">19</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-65">27</a>; its course north of<br>the Himālayas, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-65">27</a>; enters British territory as the Dihāng, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-65">27</a>; after confluence<br>with the Dibāng and Luhit known as<br>Brahmaputra, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-65">27</a>; in Assam and Bengal, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-65">27</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-66">28</a>; junction with Ganges and<br>Meghnā, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-63">25</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-66">28</a>; their combined delta<br>and estuaries, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-65">27</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-66">28</a>; its crops and<br>traffic, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-66">28</a>; not utilized for irrigation, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-66">28</a>;<br>general characters of a deltaic river,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-63">25</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-66">28</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Brahmaputra, valley of, important as<br>highway to Tibet and China, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-57">19</a>; its<br>agricultural possibilities, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-57">19</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-58">20</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Brāhui, ethnology of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-331">293</a>; ethnography<br>of the Brāhui tribe, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-348">310</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-420">382</a>; social<br>relations with the Baloch and Jats,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-368">330</a>; their Dravidian language, Brāhuī,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-420">382</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Buddhism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-446">408</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-451">413</a>; originally a system<br>of monasticism and begging friars,<br>itself a development of last and ascetic<br>stages of Brāhmanism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-446">408</a>; subsequent antagonism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-446">408</a>; its relations to<br>caste then and now, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-446">408</a>; its ethics<br>not new, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-447">409</a>; not concerned with<br>theology and psychology, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-447">409</a>; its way<br>of salvation, by purity and love, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-447">409</a>;<br>its pessimism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-447">409</a>; Nirvāna, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-448">410</a>; the<br>causes of its spread: its freedom from<br>ritual, its practical benevolence, the<br>personality of the master, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-448">410</a>; the<br>divinity of Buddha not an original<br>belief, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-448">410</a>; the influence of the Sangha<br>or Congregation of the Monastic Order,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-448">410</a>; its rules of life finally more burdensome than caste, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-448">410</a>; Buddhism as<br>a state religion under Asoka, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-449">411</a>; falling<br>off in spirituality, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-449">411</a>; as a missionary<br>religion reaching Ceylon, Burma, Siam,</p><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-578"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="578" data-folio="540" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/imperialgazette01hunt/page/n577_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 540">[p. 540]</button></div><p class="index-entry">China, Japan, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-449">411</a>; later Indian Buddhism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-449">411</a>; council of King Kanishka,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-449">411</a>; its decay and its causes, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-449">411</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-451">413</a>;<br>its idealism, and the reform of Brāhmanism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-450">412</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-451">413</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-459">421</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-460">422</a>; the rise of Muslim power, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-450">412</a>; its exclusion of the laity<br>from its Sangha, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-453">415</a>; modern Buddhism in India, its habitats and numbers,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-451">413</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-511">473</a>; survivals of Buddhism in<br>Bengal, the Sarāks, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-451">413</a>; contrasted<br>with Jainism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-452">414</a>; the religion of 3<br>per cent. of the population, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-509">471</a>; mostly<br>found in Burma and Himālayas, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-511">473</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Buffalo, wild and tame, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-269">231</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-270">232</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Bulbuls (birds), <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-279">241</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Burma, physical aspects of Northern or<br>Upper, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-58">20</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-59">21</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-237">199</a>; of Lower, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-59">21</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-239">201</a>;<br>obstacles to railway communication<br>with Upper Assam, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-58">20</a>; the Irrawaddy<br>river, its rise, affluents, climate and<br>products of its valley, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-59">21</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Burma, geology of, the Purāna group, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-100">62</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-101">63</a>;<br>older Palaeozoic in Upper, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-105">67</a>; Carbo-Trias, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-108">70</a>; Permo-Carboniferous, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-112">74</a>;<br>serpentines and jadeites, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-127">89</a>; Miocene,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-131">93</a>; Tertiary records, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-132">94</a>; table of<br>Tertiary formations, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-133">95</a>; Miocene Yenangyaung series, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-132">94</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-133">95</a>; Irrawaddy<br>system, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-135">97</a>; fossil wood, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-135">97</a>; probable<br>equivalence with Siwālik series, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-135">97</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Burma, meteorology of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-152">114</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-157">119</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-160">122</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-166">128</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-168">130</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-173">135</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-175">137</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Burma, botany of Burmese Region, by far<br>the richest of British India, and the least<br>known, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-234">196</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-243">205</a>; forests, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-235">197</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-237">199</a>; of<br>Northern Burma, like Eastern Himālayan, but without alpine zone, 199-<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-238">200</a>; cholera statistics of troops and<br>prisoners in Bengal, the 'home' of the<br>disease, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-572">534</a>.</p><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-580"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="580" data-folio="542" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/imperialgazette01hunt/page/n579_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 542">[p. 542]</button></div><p class="index-entry">Chotā Nāgpur, its population mainly<br>Dravidian, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-334">296</a>; the principal home<br>of the Mundā languages, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-421">383</a>; botany<br>of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-228">190</a>–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-230">192</a>, <em>see</em> Deccan; the Dravidian<br>tribe, as found in, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-346">308</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-347">309</a>; Kurukh,<br>the vernacular of a Dravidian tribe in<br>it and neighbouring parts of Central<br>Provinces, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-419">381</a>; efforts of missionaries<br>among its forest tribes, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-481">443</a>; its low<br>population and density, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-490">452</a>; extensive<br>emigration from, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-505">467</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-506">468</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Christianity, began with Syrian Church<br>in Malabar, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-479">441</a>; a Nestorian Church<br>under the Patriarch of Babylon, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-479">441</a>;<br>embraces the Roman communion at<br>Synod of Diamper, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-479">441</a>; later schism<br>and origin of existing branches, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-479">441</a>;<br>the Old Church, under Patriarch of<br>Antioch, the New or Jacobites affiliated<br>to Rome, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-480">442</a>; Catholic and Protestant<br>missions, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-480">442</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-481">443</a>; remarkable progress of, during the last thirty years,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-483">445</a>; its causes, religious, moral, social,<br>and political, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-483">445</a>; the social promotion<br>of the low-caste man who becomes a<br>Christian, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-483">445</a>; the religion of 1 per<br>cent. of the population, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-509">471</a>. <em>See also</em><br>Missions.</p><p class="index-entry">Christians, their sects, numbers, nationality, and distribution, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-481">443</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-482">444</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-513">475</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-514">476</a>; years<br>in that period of excess of deaths over<br>births, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-550">512</a>; apparent progressive rise<br>in high death-rates explained, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-551">513</a>;<br>the influence of extraordinary causes,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-551">513</a>; moral and physical characteristics of SE. India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-84">46</a>; geology of: the Deccan trap, its igneous<br>origin, extent, depth, and composition, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-40">2</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-125">87</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-126">88</a>; probable date of its formation,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-126">88</a>; conceals ultra-basic rocks below,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-126">88</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-127">89</a>; Madras,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-496">458</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Dhārwārian system, the: rocks of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-98">60</a>;<br>rich in iron, copper, and gold, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-98">60</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Dill, Professor, <em>Roman Society in the</em><br><em>Last Days of the Western Empire</em>,<br>quoted, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-381">343</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-382">344</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Dogs (wild), two species, differ in dentition from wolves and jackals, their<br>distribution and habits, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-259">221</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-260">222</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Dolphin, the Gangetic, or <em>sūsū</em>; of the<br>Indus, Ganges, and Brahmaputra and<br>their tributaries, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-276">238</a>; blind, one of<br>three surviving representatives of family<br>of Platanistidae, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-276">238</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Doms, Haris, Chamārs, Muchis, Bauris,<br>castes of the seventh and lowest class<br>in Bengal, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-366">328</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Dravidian: the geological era, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-102">64</a>–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-105">67</a>; Sir W. W. Hunter's<br>theory, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-336">298</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-337">299</a>; two different families of<br>languages spoken by the race, Dravidian<br>proper and Mundā, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-417">379</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Dravidian Languages, the, their main<br>characteristics, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-417">379</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-418">380</a>;
