pressure, weather, and rainfall, in different<br>parts of India from October, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-173">135</a>–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-175">137</a>;<br>mean rainfall, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-178">140</a>.</p><h3 class="sec-h2">E.</h3><p class="index-entry">Eagles, the golden eagle, the imperial,<br>the spotted, the crested serpent, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-291">253</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Earthquakes, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-136">98</a>; great earthquake of<br>Assam in 1897, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-136">98</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-137">99</a>; of Kangra in<br>1905, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-136">98</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-137">99</a>; of Cutch in 1819, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-137">99</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Education, statistics of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-521">483</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-522">484</a>; proportion of literates, who can read and<br>write, to illiterates, in India, 1 to 10<br>of males, 1 to 144 of females, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-521">483</a>; in<br>the Provinces, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-521">483</a>; highest in Burma,<br>due to free education by the <em>pongyis</em><br>or Buddhist monks, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-521">483</a>; proportion<br>highest along and near sea-coast, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-522">484</a>;<br>higher among Dravidians and Mongolians, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-522">484</a>; proportion among religious<br>communities, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-522">484</a>; highest among<br>Pārsīs, 66 per cent., lowest among<br>Animists, ½ per cent., <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-522">484</a>; proportion<br>of literates in English, 68 males and<br>7 females out of every 10,000 of each<br>sex, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-522">484</a>; highest among Pārsīs, lowest among Animists, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-522">484</a>. <em>See</em> Census<br>Table IX, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-533">495</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Elephants, wild and tamed, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-268">230</a>; a single<br>species surviving of the eleven of<br>Pliocene times, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-135">97</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Emigration from India, statistics of, incomplete, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-508">470</a>; mainly of labourers<br>and coolies to the British Colonies,<br>including South Africa, Uganda, Mauritius, and British Guiana, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-509">471</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-510">472</a>;<br>conditions of coolie recruitment, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-509">471</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Endogamy, tribal, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-346">308</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-347">309</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-348">310</a>; of<br>caste-system, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-325">287</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-349">311</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-355">317</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-360">322</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-372">334</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-373">335</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-386">348</a>; of Muhammadans, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-367">329</a>;<br>Greek, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-378">340</a>; Roman, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-378">340</a>; under later<br>Roman Empire, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-381">343</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-382">344</a>; part of the<br>pride of race and colour everywhere,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-383">345</a>; probably later in time and thought<br>than exogamy, the practice of primitive<br>tribes, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-382">344</a>.</p><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-582"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="582" data-folio="544" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/imperialgazette01hunt/page/n581_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 544">[p. 544]</button></div><p class="index-entry">English Church in India, succeeded to<br>Lutheran missions, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-481">443</a>; foundation of<br>see of Calcutta, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-481">443</a>; Heber, second<br>bishop, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-481">443</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Enteric fever, dependent, like cholera,<br>upon pollution of water-supply by first<br>rains after drought, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-562">524</a>; death-rate<br>from, among European troops, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-565">527</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-566">528</a>; in Native army, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-568">530</a>; in jails, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-569">531</a>;<br>relation of death-rate to age, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-566">528</a>;<br>European troops eighty-eight times more<br>liable to this disease than native, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-568">530</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Eranian (or Iranian) languages, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-391">353</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-394">356</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Estuarial Flora, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-220">182</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Ethnography. <em>See</em> Ethnology and Caste, B.</p><p class="index-entry">Ethnology and Caste, ch. <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/02-general-preface/#page-12">vi</a>, pp. <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-321">283</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-386">348</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">A. The data of ethnology or science<br>of racial divisions, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-321">283</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-346">308</a>; physical<br>characters most trustworthy, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-321">283</a>; indefinite physical characters, colour of<br>skin, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-321">283</a>; hair and eyes, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-322">284</a>; definite<br>or anthropometric physical characters,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-322">284</a>; superiority of anthropometry, or<br>measurements of the living, over craniometry, or measurements of skulls, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-322">284</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-323">285</a>; anthropometry in India as part<br>of Ethnographic Survey, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-323">285</a>; the data<br>now available, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-324">286</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-325">287</a>; the establish-<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-331">293</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-337">299</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-341">303</a>; its uniformity and its<br>identity, in spite of social differences,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-331">293</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-337">299</a>; its non-Indian origin, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-338">300</a>; the<br>mode of its entry into India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-339">301</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-341">303</a>;<br>possibly by a series of tribal migrations<br>from the north-west, first of men with<br>their wives, under favourable climatic<br>conditions no longer existing, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-68">30</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-339">301</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-340">302</a>; probable decrease in modern<br>times of rainfall and fertility in Central<br>Asia and Persia, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-339">301</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-340">302</a>; (3) Aryo-Dravidian or Hindustāni type, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-332">294</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-341">303</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-342">304</a>; Dr. Hoernle's theory of its<br>origin from secondary migrations of<br>men without their women, confirmed<br>by Dr. Grierson's Linguistic Survey,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-341">303</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-342">304</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-396">358</a>; (4) the Mongolo-Dravidian or Bengali type, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-332">294</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-342">304</a>;<br>(5) the Scytho-Dravidian type, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-331">293</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-342">304</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-346">308</a>; historical record of Scythian<br>invasions of India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-343">305</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-344">306</a>; possible<br>origin of Scytho-Dravidian type, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-344">306</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-345">307</a>; possible course of Scythians from<br>Western Punjab southwards, to become<br>ancestors of Marāthās, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-345">307</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-346">308</a>; (6)<br>the Turko-Irānian type, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-331">293</a>; (7) the<br>Mongoloid type, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-333">295</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-334">296</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">B. Ethnography, the data of, or social ment of three main types, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-324">286</a>; this<br>classification accepted by independent<br>authorities in England, France, and<br>Germany, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-324">286</a>; confirmed by craniometry, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-324">286</a>; conditions favourable to<br>anthropometry in India, especially<br>prohibition of mixed marriages by law<br>of caste, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-324">286</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-326">288</a>; measurement of<br>head form, the cephalic index, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-326">288</a>;<br>division of heads into broad, long, and<br>medium, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-326">288</a>; value of cephalic index<br>as a test of race, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-326">288</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-327">289</a>; head-form<br>in India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-327">289</a>; measurement of the nose,<br>the nasal index, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-327">289</a>; division of noses<br>into narrow, broad, and medium, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-327">289</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-328">290</a>; value of nasal index as a test of<br>racial affinity, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-328">290</a>; the nasal index in<br>India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-328">290</a>; its correspondence with<br>social groupings, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-328">290</a>; the orbito-nasal<br>index, a test of Mongolian affinities,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-329">291</a>; division of faces into platyopic,<br>mesopic, and pro-opic, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-329">291</a>; stature,<br>as an index of race, more significant<br>in India than in Europe, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-330">292</a>; the<br>seven main physical types in India<br>(exclusive of Negritos), their characteristics and geographical distribution, 292–<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-335">297</a>; limitations of the type scheme,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-335">297</a>; (1) the Dravidian type, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-334">296</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-336">298</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-337">299</a>; probably the oldest indigenous, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-336">298</a>; of doubtful origin and<br>affinities, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-336">298</a>; Huxley's surmise, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-336">298</a>;<br>Sir W. Hunter's theory, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-336">298</a>; (2) the<br>Indo-Aryan type, its physical and<br>social characteristics and distribution, divisions, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-346">308</a>–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-385">347</a>; the Dravidian tribe,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-346">308</a>; the Mongoloid tribe, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-347">309</a>; the<br>Turko-Irānian tribes; the Afghān type,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-347">309</a>; the Baloch and Brāhui type, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-348">310</a>;<br>the word 'caste,' its derivation and<br>implication, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-349">311</a>; definition of caste,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-349">311</a>; conversion of tribes into castes<br>with and without mixture of blood, by<br>absorption into old castes, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-349">311</a>; as a<br>result of social ambition, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-350">312</a>; as a<br>result of religious conversion, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-350">312</a>; by<br>creation of new castes with adoption of<br>ritual and customs, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-350">312</a>; by a gradual<br>and insensible conversion to Hinduism<br>with retention of tribal designation,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-351">313</a>; types of caste, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-351">313</a>–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-360">322</a>; tribal<br>castes, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-352">314</a>; functional or occupational<br>castes, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-352">314</a>; sectarian castes, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-353">315</a>;<br>castes formed by crossing, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-354">316</a>; the<br>Shāgirdpeshas, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-355">317</a>; national castes,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-356">318</a>; the Marāthās, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-356">318</a>; castes of<br>degradation and promotion formed by<br>change of residence, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-357">319</a>; the Nambūdri Brāhmans of Malabar, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-357">319</a>; the<br>Rārhi Brāhmans of Bengal, the Barwaiks in Chānda, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-357">319</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-358">320</a>; castes<br>formed by changes of custom, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-359">321</a>;<br>totemism and totemistic exogamy,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-360">322</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-361">323</a>; classification of castes, its<br>difficulties for the Census, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-361">323</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-362">324</a>;<br>principles adopted in the 1901 Census,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-362">324</a>; by recognized social precedence,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-362">324</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-363">325</a>; general results, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-363">325</a>; persistent influence of traditional system of<br>four original castes, the water and <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-583" data-page="583" data-folio="545" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/imperialgazette01hunt/page/n582_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 545">[p. 545]</button> sweetmeat test, range of pollution, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-363">325</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-364">326</a>; the seven main classes of Hindus in Bengal, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-364">326</a>–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-366">328</a>; (1) Brāhmans of various status, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-364">326</a>; (2) Rājputs, Baidyas, Kāyasths, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-365">327</a>; (3) Navasākha and other ‘clean’ Sūdras, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-365">327</a>; (4) Chāsi, Kaibartta, and Goāla, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-365">327</a>; (5) whom the village barber will shave, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-366">328</a>; (6) whom the regular barber will not shave, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-366">328</a>; (7) eaters of all manner of unclean food, scavengers, &amp;c., <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-366">328</a>; opposition between Hinduism and Islām, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-366">328</a>; modified by contagion of caste in India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-367">329</a>; exaltation of foreign descent in both, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-367">329</a>; Muhammadan practice of hypergamy and endogamy, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-367">329</a>; absence of caste system in Baluchistān and Burma, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-367">329</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-368">330</a>; distribution of nearly 2,400 castes and tribes, <em>see</em> Ethnographic Map in Atlas; <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-53">15</a>,000,000 Brāhmans, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-369">331</a>; origin of caste, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-370">332</a>–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-385">347</a>; the Indian theory of four original castes as contained in Institutes of Manu, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-370">332</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-371">333</a>; its historic elements stated, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-371">333</a>, and confirmed by Dr. Fick’s studies, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-372">334</a>; its probable origin in the Irānian legend of four classes, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-372">334</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-373">335</a>; modified by existence of racial antagonism in India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-373">335</a>, and by later developments, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-374">336</a>; Sir Denzil Ibbetson’s theory of evolution of caste, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-374">336</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-375">337</a>; M. Senart’s criticism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-375">337</a>; Mr. Nesfield’s theory, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-375">337</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-376">338</a>; M. Senart’s theory, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-377">339</a>–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-380">342</a>; caste an extension of ancient Aryan family system, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-378">340</a>; early Greek and Roman parallels, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-378">340</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-379">341</a>; explanation of later divergence in India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-379">341</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-380">342</a>; caste not merely community of occupation, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-380">342</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-382">344</a>; not comparable with guilds of mediaeval Europe, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-380">342</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-381">343</a>; caste tendencies under the later Roman Empire, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-381">343</a>; public functions made hereditary guilds with endogamy for men, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-381">343</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-382">344</a>; did not survive Empire, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-382">344</a>; castes not merely developed tribes, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-382">344</a>; primitive tribes exogamous, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-382">344</a>; the genesis of caste, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-383">345</a>–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-385">347</a>; from a basis of widespread fact, observed in India and elsewhere, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-383">345</a>; distinction of blood plus colour between higher and lower races in contact, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-383">345</a>; position of half-breeds, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-383">345</a>; caste a result of second and successive waves of Indo-Aryan invaders, and of hypergamy, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-383">345</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-384">346</a>; superstructure of fiction, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-384">346</a>; social or artificial distinctions tend to be thought natural and genetic, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-384">346</a>; the caste instinct favoured by characteristics of Indian intellect, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-385">347</a>; in agreement with philosophic doctrine of transmigration and <em>karma</em>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-385">347</a>; summary of conclusions, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-385">347</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-386">348</a>; bibliography, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-386">348</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Eurasians and Europeans, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-515">477</a>; their numbers, growth, and distribution, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-515">477</a>; apparent more rapid growth of Eurasian population explained, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-515">477</a>; two-fifths of Europeans born in India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-515">477</a>; ten-elevenths of Europeans British subjects, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-515">477</a>; religions of Europeans and Eurasians, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-515">477</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Exogamy, tribal, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-346">308</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-347">309</a>; influence of south-west<br>monsoons upon climate of its basin, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-60">22</a>;<br>its river system, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-61">23</a>–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-64">26</a>; its sanctity, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-61">23</a>;<br>the fertilizer and highway of Bengal,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-60">22</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-61">23</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-62">24</a>; influence of, upon meteorology, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-150">112</a>; botany of, <em>see</em> Malabar.</p><p class="index-entry">Ghāts, Eastern, along the east coast,<br>physical aspects of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-79">41</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-80">42</a>; rivers of<br>the Eastern Ghāts, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-80">42</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-81">43</a>; geology of,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-79">41</a>; botany of, <em>see</em> Deccan.</p><p class="index-entry">Ghāts, Southern, divide Tinnevelly and<br>Madura from Travancore, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-84">46</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Goālā, sub-caste of fourth class in Bengal,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-365">327</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Goats (wild), chiefly Himālayan, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-271">233</a>;<br>the Asiatic ibex, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-271">233</a>; the Persian wild<br>goat or Sind ibex, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-271">233</a>; the Mārkhor,<br>the finest of wild goats, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-271">233</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-272">234</a>; the<br>Nīlgiri wild goat or ibex of sportsmen, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-272">234</a>; goat antelopes, the Serow,<br>the Gural, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-272">234</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Godāvari, the, a principal river of the<br>Deccan, its rise, course, affluents, ‘anicut,’ and delta, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-82">44</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-83">45</a>; its valley,<br>geology of: fossil remains of <em>Ceratodus</em><br>and other fish and reptiles, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-122">84</a>; pleistocene alluvium, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-138">100</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Gonds, the: a Dravidian tribe, in the<br>‘stone’ age, their home, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-82">44</a>; their debasement of the pure religion of the<br>Satnāmis to their own level, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-466">428</a>; their<br>language, of many dialects, unwritten<br>and without a literature, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-419">381</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Gondwāna system, of sub-aerial and<br>fresh-water deposits, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-40">2</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-118">80</a>–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-125">87</a>; from<br>Carboniferous through Permian and<br>Triassic times, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-119">81</a>; its distribution, 81–<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-122">84</a>; its division into Lower and Upper,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-121">83</a>, their subdivisions into Tālcher, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-120">82</a>;<br>Dāmuda, coal-bearing, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-121">83</a>; character of its fossil plants, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-122">84</a>; the Glossopteris flora, its resemblance to fossil plants of similar formations in Australia, Africa, and South America, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-122">84</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-123">85</a>; explained by an old Indo-African continent, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-123">85</a>; further evidence of its former existence, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-123">85</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-125">87</a>; broken up in early Tertiary times, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-125">87</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Goshawk, wild and tamed, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-292">254</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Greek and Roman parallels to system of caste, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-378">340</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-379">341</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-381">343</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-382">344</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Gujarāt, great variability of rainfall, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-180">142</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-183">145</a>; botany of Southern, <em>see</em> Malabar; its language, Gujarātī, a midland speech, with its own written character, and a flourishing literature, old and new, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-406">368</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-407">369</a>; population and density, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-490">452</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Guru Nānak, the founder of Sikhism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-464">426</a>.</p><h4 class="sec-h3">H.</h4><p class="index-entry">Haimantas of Upper Cambrian system, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-103">65</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-104">66</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Hanumāns or Langurs, monkeys, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-254">216</a>; tropical zone, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-210">172</a>; temperate zone, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-211">173</a>; alpine zone, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-213">175</a>; Tibetan valleys, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-213">175</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-214">176</a>; zoology of Higher Himālayas, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-252">214</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Hindī, laxly used to denote rural dialects of Bihārī, and Eastern and Western Hindī, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-402">364</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Hindī: Western: the modern vernacular of the old Midland, of the Gangetic Doāb, and northwards, now and formerly the most important of all Indian languages, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-403">365</a>; its principal dialects, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-404">366</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-405">367</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Hindī: Eastern: meeting of languages of the Midland and Outer Band, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-407">369</a>; the language of Oudh, Baghelkhand, and Chhattīsgarh in the Central Provinces, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-407">369</a>; in an early form the sacred language of Jainism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-407">369</a>; in its present form the language of nearly all epic poetry of Hindustān, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-408">370</a>; its wide distribution, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-408">370</a>; its great and popular literature and its influence upon rustic speech, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-408">370</a>; high qualities of the language and literature of Eastern and Western Hindī, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-405">367</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Hindostānī, the principal dialect of Western Hindī, a local vernacular and also a <em>lingua franca</em> of north and west continental India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-403">365</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-404">366</a>; spread from Delhi by the Mughal Empire, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-403">365</a>; Hindī Hindostānī, its artificial origin, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-404">366</a>; its popularity and general adoption (with Urdū) in Upper India for prose writing, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-404">366</a>; written in the Deva-nāgarī character, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-404">366</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Hinduism, the present creed of the vast majority, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-455">417</a>; evolved from a reformed Brāhmanism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-455">417</a>; creation of a national ideal of worship, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-457">419</a>; due to religious influence of the epics, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-457">419</a>; the Mahābhārata, its religion and legends of heroes and heroines, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-456">418</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-457">419</a>; the Rāmāyana, Rāma, and Sītā models of holiness, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-456">418</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-457">419</a>; Brāhmanism modified by non-Aryan forms of belief, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-455">417</a>; Siva, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-457">419</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-458">420</a>; compared with Vishnu, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-458">420</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-459">421</a>; the extension of Sivaism by Kumārila Bhatta and Sankarāchārya, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-459">421</a>; the Saiva sects, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-459">421</a>–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-461">423</a>; the Smārtas, Brāhmans of Madras and S. Deccan, a sect of highest pantheism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-459">421</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-460">422</a>; the Lingāyats, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-460">422</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-461">423</a>; Vishnu, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-461">423</a>; his Avatāras or incarnations, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-461">423</a>; the gods of Vaishnavism, Krishna, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-461">423</a>; the god of the middle class, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-462">424</a>; Rāma, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-462">424</a>; the growth of Vaishnavism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-462">424</a>; the Vaishnava reformers, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-463">425</a>; Kabīr and Kabīrpanthis, the link between Hinduism and Islām, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-463">425</a>; and inspirer of Sikhism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-463">425</a>; the preacher, Chaitanya, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-464">426</a>; erotic Vaishnavism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-464">426</a>; Sikhism, founded by Guru Nānak, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-464">426</a>; condemns polytheism, superstition, caste, and Brāhmanical supremacy, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-464">426</a>; its tendency<br>to relapse into Hinduism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-465">427</a>; the<br>Sāktas, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-465">427</a>; reformed sects of modern<br>Vaishnavism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-465">427</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-468">430</a>; the Rādhāswāmīs, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-465">427</a>; sects founded on social<br>revolt, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-466">428</a>; the Satnāmis, their fall<br>from original creed, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-466">428</a>; modern theistic sects, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-467">429</a>; eclecticism of Hindu<br>sectarianism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-468">430</a>; the religion of<br>70 per cent. of the population, of<br>207 millions, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-509">471</a>; everywhere predominant, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-510">472</a>; yields to Buddhism in<br>Burma, to Islām in NW. India and E.<br>Bengal, to Animism in hills of Assam,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-510">472</a>; its losses and gains, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-510">472</a>; its<br>teaching and customs as bearing upon<br>moral and physical vigour, fertility,<br>and sanitation, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-510">472</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-538">500</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-539">501</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-540">502</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-545">507</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-546">508</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-558">520</a>. <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-587" data-page="587" data-folio="549" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/imperialgazette01hunt/page/n586_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 549">[p. 549]</button></p><p class="index-entry">Hindu Kush, the, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-48">10</a>; ‘a highway of<br>nations,’ 12; the parting point of<br>Indo-Aryans from Iranians, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-391">353</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Hippopotamus, bones of, in Narbadā<br>alluvium, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-137">99</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Hoernle, Dr., his <em>Grammar of Eastern</em><br><em>Hindi</em>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-387">349</a>; his theory of origin of<br>a double Aryan migration into India,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-341">303</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-342">304</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-396">358</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Holarctic or Palaearctic (zoological)<br>Region, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-252">214</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Honey Guides (birds), <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-285">247</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Hot season, of March, April, and May,<br>changes of pressure and air movements,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-153">115</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-154">116</a>; storms of local causation,<br>their distribution, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-155">117</a>; mean rainfall,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-178">140</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Houses and house room, the different<br>meanings attached in Census to the<br>term ‘house,’ 458; either an enclosure,<br>occupied by one family or several, or<br>an enclosure the residence of a single<br>commensal family, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-497">459</a>; average number of persons per house, high in Punjab<br>and Kashmīr (6), elsewhere very uniform, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-496">458</a>; overcrowding in, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-542">504</a>. <em>See</em><br>Census Table I.</p><p class="index-entry">Human sacrifice, among the Khonds,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-359">321</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-443">405</a>; among the early Indo-Aryans, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-443">405</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-444">406</a>; and even now to the<br>goddess Kālī, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-444">406</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">‘Humming-birds,’ so called, or sun-birds,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-284">246</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Hyderābād (Nizam’s Dominions), ethnology of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-334">296</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Hyena, the striped, common throughout<br>the Peninsula, its haunts and habits,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-258">220</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Hypergamy or ‘marrying-up,’ a relaxation of caste system, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-338">300</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-350">312</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-356">318</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-357">319</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-383">345</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-386">348</a>; of higher Muhammadans, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-367">329</a>; part of the Indian theory<br>of the origin of caste, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-370">332</a>.</p><h4 class="sec-h3">I.</h4><p class="index-entry">Ibbetson, Sir Denzil’s theory of the evolution of caste, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-374">336</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-375">337</a>; M. Senart’s<br>criticism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-375">337</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Ibex: the Asiatic, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-271">233</a>; ‘Sind ibex,’<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-271">233</a>; the Mārkhor, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-271">233</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-272">234</a>;
