of Mongoloids, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-333">295</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-334">296</a>; from NW. China<br>down the river valleys to Burma and<br>Assam, and up the Brahmaputra into<br>Tibet, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-422">384</a>; in three successive waves,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-423">385</a>; the Muhammadan invasions and<br>conquests of India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-471">433</a>; their influence<br>upon its religious history, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-471">433</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-472">434</a>;<br>indifference or toleration of Mughal<br>emperors towards Hinduism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-472">434</a>; and<br>Catholicism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-480">442</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Iranian (or Eranian) Languages, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-391">353</a>–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-394">356</a>;<br>the Turko-Iranian type, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-331">293</a>; tribes,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-347">309</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-348">310</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Irrawaddy (geological) system, the, of<br>folded pliocene rocks, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-132">94</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-133">95</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Irrawaddy River, the, its rise, course, and<br>affluents, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-58">20</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-59">21</a>; its valley, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-59">21</a>; its<br>delta, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-59">21</a>; meteorology of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-160">122</a>; botany<br>of, <em>see</em> Sundarbans.</p><p class="index-entry">Irrigation, in Afghānistān, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-50">12</a>; at Hardwār on Ganges, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-61">23</a>; from Ganges and<br>Jumna, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-62">24</a>; its influence upon their<br>volume, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-62">24</a>; dams or ‘anecuts’ of the<br>Godāvari, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-83">45</a>; of the Kistna, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-83">45</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-507">469</a>;<br>of the Cauvery, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-83">45</a>; renders crops independent of rainfall, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-179">141</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-489">451</a>; great<br>recent development of, from the Chenāb, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-503">465</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-506">468</a>; an incentive to internal<br>migration, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-506">468</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-507">469</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Islām, opposition between, and Hinduism,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-366">328</a>; modified by contagion of caste,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-367">329</a>; its early contact with Hinduism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-471">433</a>; sufferings of Brāhmanism and<br>Buddhism from Muslim invaders and<br>raiders from the tenth to the sixteenth<br>century, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-471">433</a>; general tolerance of the<br>Muhammadan rulers, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-472">434</a>; Aurangzeb<br>and Tipū Sultān exceptions to rule,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-472">434</a>; power and progress of Islām,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-472">434</a>; due to its own vitality rather than<br>to conversion, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-473">435</a>; the regions of its<br>strength, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-472">434</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-473">435</a>; increase in Eastern<br>Bengal, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-473">435</a>; the Musalmān healthier,<br>better fed, and more reproductive than<br>the Hindu, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-473">435</a>; its democracy an<br>attraction to low-caste men, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-473">435</a>; its<br>debt to Animism and Hinduism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-473">435</a>;<br>its fusion with Animism in the Pachpiriyas, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-473">435</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-474">436</a>; sects of Islām, 436–<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-476">438</a>; Sunnis and Shiahs, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-474">436</a>; minor<br>sects: the Wahābis, puritanical and<br>aggressive, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-474">436</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-475">437</a>; Sūfiism, a combination of Aryan pantheism with<br>Semitic monotheism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-475">437</a>; the Ahmadiyas, the claim of their leader to be at<br>once Mahdi and Messiah, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-476">438</a>; the Moplahs, the Bohras, and the Khojas, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-476">438</a>;<br>the revival of Islām in Northern India,<br>cheap circulation of sacred books, and<br>desire for education, Anglo-Oriental<br>College at Alīgarh, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-476">438</a>. <em>See also</em><br>Muhammadanism.</p><h3 class="sec-h2">J.</h3><p class="index-entry">Jackals, very widely distributed, their<br>habits and cry, breed freely with dogs,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-259">221</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Jacobābād, highest temperatures recorded<br>at, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-187">149</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Jagannāth, temple of, in Orissa, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-64">26</a>; one<br>of the most sacred shrines of Hinduism,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-64">26</a>; its worship a coalition of Buddhist<br>and Brāhman doctrines, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-64">26</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Jainism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-452">414</a>–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-455">417</a>; its founder Vardhamāna<br>of the same country, class, and time as<br>Buddha, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-452">414</a>; compared and contrasted<br>with Buddhism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-452">414</a>; the resemblance<br>of their moral rules, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-452">414</a>; it lays<br>greater stress upon asceticism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-452">414</a>; the<br>schism of the ‘white-clothed’ from<br>‘those clothed with the sky,’ 414, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-453">415</a>;<br>its former political influence and<br>splendid temples, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-453">415</a>; the causes of<br>its survival to the present day, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-453">415</a>; its<br>vast literature only partially explored,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-453">415</a>; its pantheon, a body of deified<br>saints, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-453">415</a>; the habits of its monks,<br>and their extreme regard for animal<br>life, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-454">416</a>; its sanctuaries and splendid<br>temples, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-454">416</a>; its close resemblance to<br>Hinduism, with more than its caste<br>exclusiveness, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-454">416</a>; points of difference,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-454">416</a>; its boundless charity, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-455">417</a>; decreasing number of its adherents, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-454">416</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-455">417</a>; its principal habitats, the cities <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-589" data-page="589" data-folio="551" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/imperialgazette01hunt/page/n588_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 551">[p. 551]</button> and marts of Western India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-454">416</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-511">473</a>;<br>its three sects, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-455">417</a>; its sacred language,<br>an early form of Eastern Hindī, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-407">369</a>.<br><em>See also</em> Svetāmbara, Pinjrapols.</p><p class="index-entry">Jaipur, meteorology of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-164">126</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Jaisalmer, Jurassic of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-114">76</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Jāts or Jats, representatives of Indo-Aryan type, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-331">293</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-337">299</a>; intermarriage<br>with Rājputs, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-339">301</a>; practise widow<br>remarriage, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-360">322</a>; in Baluchistān, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-368">330</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Jesuit missions. <em>See</em> Missions.</p><p class="index-entry">Jews, very early settlers in India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-479">441</a>;<br>their colonies at Kolāba in Bombay,<br>and Cochin on the Malabar coast, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-479">441</a>;<br>their partial amalgamation with the<br>native races, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-479">441</a>; recent increase not<br>due to immigration, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-479">441</a>; their number,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-531">493</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Jhelum, the river of vale of Kashmir, its<br>course, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-69">31</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-70">32</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Jumna, the, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-61">23</a>; its rise and independent<br>course, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-62">24</a>; its confluence with the<br>Ganges, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-62">24</a>; the Prayāg, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-62">24</a>; its canals,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-62">24</a>.</p><h3 class="sec-h2">K.</h3><p class="index-entry">Kabīr, a Vaishnava reformer (1380–<br>1420 <span class="sc">A. D.</span>), <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-463">425</a>; a weaver by caste,<br>taught the spiritual equality of all men,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-463">425</a>; difference in rank, caste, and<br>religion, and the accidents of life are<br>Māya, or illusion, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-463">425</a>; happiness<br>comes by fervent faith (<em>bhakti</em>) and<br>meditation on the Godhead, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-463">425</a>; a link<br>between Islām and Hinduism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-463">425</a>; the<br>inspirer of Sikhism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-463">425</a>; and founder of<br>sect of Kabīrpanthis, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-463">425</a>; now reverting<br>to idolatry, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-463">425</a>; their later apostle,<br>Chaitanya (1485–1527 <span class="sc">A. D.</span>), <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-464">426</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Kābul, physical href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-462">424</a>; head-quarters of his cult at<br> Muttra, on the upper Jumna, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-462">424</a>; its<br> luxurious ceremonial and lax morality,<br> <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-462">424</a>; its grosser<br> developments in the Vallabhāchārya<br> sect, four original<br> castes or groups, the warrior class, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-370">332</a>;<br> their suppression by the Brahmāns,<br> <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-445">407</a>; held superior to Brahmāns in<br> Magadha, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-446">408</a>; their rejection from<br> ascetic fraternities possibly contributed<br> to rise of Buddhism and Jainism in<br> Magadha, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-446">408</a>; involved in the struggle<br> against Buddhism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-460">422</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Kuki-Chin group of Tibeto-Burman<br> languages, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-425">387</a>; spoken in Manipur and<br> Cāchār, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-425">387</a>; the most typical of their<br> family, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-426">388</a>; Meithei, the official<br> language, alone has alphabet and<br> literature, <a href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-356">318</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-357">319</a> ; their language, Marāthī, <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-592" data-page="592" data-folio="554" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/imperialgazette01hunt/page/n591_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 554">[p. 554]</button> spoken from Damān to beyond Goa, between the Ghāts and the Arabian Sea, in the north of the Deccan, in Berār, in the NW. of the Nizam's Dominions, in the south of the Central Provinces and in the State of Bastar, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-411">373</a>; an Outer language with three main dialects, Desī spoken round Poona and in N. and Central Konkan; Konkanī in S. Konkan; and the dialect of Berār and the Central Provinces, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-412">374</a>; its <em>l</em>-participle, found from Assam to Arabian Sea, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-411">373</a>; its copious and popular literature, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-411">373</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-412">374</a>; written in Deva-nāgarī, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-412">374</a>; spoken by more than eighteen millions, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-402">364</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Marriage, its universality as a sacred obligation, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-486">448</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-487">449</a>; comparative rate of, among females in England and India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-517">479</a>; proportion of celibates and widows among men and women in England and India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-519">481</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-546">508</a>; ages at marriage vary with religion and locality, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-519">481</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-520">482</a>; infant marriage, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-520">482</a>; prohibition of widow remarriage often discourages very early wedlock, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-520">482</a>; early marriages unusual amongst Muhammadans, Buddhists, and generally Animists, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-520">482</a>; the physical effects on the race of universal and early marriage, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-538">500</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-539">501</a>; effect on the birth-rate, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-545">507</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-546">508</a>.<br><em>See also</em> Exogamy, Endogamy, Hypergamy, Polyandry, Polygamy, Census Table VIII.</p><p class="index-entry">Mārwārī, a dialect of Rājasthānī, carried by trade all over India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-405">367</a>; its copious literature and peculiar character, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-406">368</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Mazdaism, the Pārsī religion, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-477">439</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-478">440</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Megalosaurus (<em>M. Bucklandi</em>), its occurrence in the Ariyalūr stage of Coromandel Cretaceous, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-117">79</a>; its peculiar interest and significance, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-117">79</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Meteorological Department in India, its initiation, development, present constitution, stations, and observatories, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-143">105</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-144">106</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Meteorology of India, ch. <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/01-frontmatter/#page-9">iii</a>, pp. <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-142">104</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-194">156</a>.<br>I. General meteorology of India proper with Burma and Baluchistān, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-142">104</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-175">137</a>; its singular interest due to (1) its variety and contrasts in respect of rainfall, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-142">104</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-143">105</a>; (2) its combination of tropical and temperate region conditions, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-143">105</a>; (3) its covering an area of pronounced monsoon conditions, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-143">105</a>; initiation and development of meteorological observation and inquiry in India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-143">105</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-144">106</a>; present constitution of the Meteorological department, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-144">106</a>; India not an isolated meteorological area, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-145">107</a>; physiographic features of India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-145">107</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-146">108</a>; outside influences which affect meteorological conditions of India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-147">109</a>; the alternation of seasons known as the monsoons the primary fact of Indian meteorology, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-147">109</a>; north-east or dry and south-west or wet monsoons, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-148">110</a>; subdivision of each monsoon into two periods, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-148">110</a>; pressure conditions in Asia and Indian Ocean during cold-weather period of dry monsoon, with graphic, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-148">110</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-149">111</a>; sketch of air movement in India during cold-weather period, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-149">111</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-150">112</a>; storms of the cold-weather period — phenomena of the upper current, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-150">112</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-151">113</a>; weather in India during cold-weather period, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-151">113</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-152">114</a>; precipitation of the cold-weather period, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-152">114</a>; meteorology of the second or hot half of the dry or north-east monsoon, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-153">115</a>; changes of pressure conditions and air movement accompanying local increase of temperature in Indian land area, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-154">116</a>; storms of the hot-weather period, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-155">117</a>; the wet season or south-west monsoon, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-156">118</a>; graphic of pressures, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-157">119</a>; extension of the south-east trades across the equator into the Indian sea and land areas, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-157">119</a>; cyclonic storms in Arabian Sea, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-158">120</a>; complete field of extension of the south-west monsoon currents, with graphic, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-159">121</a>; Bay of Bengal current, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-160">122</a>; Arabian Sea current, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-161">123</a>; monsoon trough of low pressure, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-162">124</a>; date of establishment of wet monsoon in different parts of India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-162">124</a>; pulsatory character of the rainfall of the period, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-162">124</a>; cyclonic storms of the rainy season in the Bay of Bengal, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-163">125</a>; normal rainfall, May to October, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-164">126</a>; humidity and temperature conditions during the south-west monsoon, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-164">126</a>; variations of the strength of the south-west monsoon currents related to corresponding variations in the south-east trades of the Indian Ocean, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-165">127</a>; rainfall in Western India and Abyssinia appears to vary similarly, while rainfall in North-Western India and Burma varies inversely, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-165">127</a>; table of variations, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-166">128</a>; variations in local extension of monsoon currents caused by pressure anomalies, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-165">127</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-166">128</a>; influence of snowfall in Himālayas on the rainfall, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-167">129</a>; peculiar character of rainfall as a discontinuous phenomenon, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-167">129</a>; important variations of monsoon rains from normal, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-168">130</a>; chart of normal rainfall in the plains, June to September, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-169">131</a>; the retreating south-west monsoon period, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-169">131</a>; gradual withdrawal of the monsoon currents from the Indian area, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-170">132</a>; pressure changes in Indian area, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-170">132</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-171">133</a>; recurvature of the Bay current in October and November towards the west, rains in Madras, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-171">133</a> ;<br>probable conditions of retreat of monsoon current in the Arabian Sea, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-172">134</a> ;<br>storms of the period, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-172">134</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-173">135</a> ; further<br>pressure and temperature changes in<br>India during period, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-173">135</a> ; this season<br>really a transitional period of considerable duration, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-174">136</a> ; precipitation of<br>period, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-174">136</a> ; summary of more important abnormal features of weather<br>and rainfall distribution of period, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-175">137</a>. <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-593" data-page="593" data-folio="555" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/imperialgazette01hunt/page/n592_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 555">[p. 555]</button></p><p class="index-entry">II. Special remarks on rainfall and<br>droughts, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-176">138</a>–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-184">146</a> ; aqueous vapour,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-176">138</a> ; rate of cooling of ascending air,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-176">138</a> ; ascensional or convective air movement the chief cause of rainfall, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-176">138</a> ;<br>ascensional movement largely due to<br>diurnal heating by solar action, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-176">138</a> ;<br>ascensional movement of humid current<br>forced up and across a line of hills,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-177">139</a> ; ascensional movement in cyclonic<br>storms, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-177">139</a> ; ascensional movement due<br>to resistance to horizontal movement at<br>the earth's surface, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-177">139</a> : distribution of<br>rainfall by season in India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-178">140</a>, and<br>Appendix A, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-191">153</a> ; cold-weather rainfall, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-178">140</a> ; due to storms, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-178">140</a> ; hot-weather rainfall, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-179">141</a> ; rainfall of the<br>wet or south-west monsoon, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-179">141</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-180">142</a> ;<br>action of forced ascent of aqueous<br>vapour, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-180">142</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-181">143</a> ; of cyclonic storms,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-181">143</a> ; examples of excessive downpours<br>in twenty-four hours during the wet<br>monsoon, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-181">143</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-182">144</a> ; the labours of St. Francis<br>Xavier in S. Madras and the Malabar<br>coast (1542–52), <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-480">442</a> ; his death in<br>China and burial in the Church of Bom<br>Jesus at Goa, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-480">442</a> ; the conciliatory and<br>popular system of his followers, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-480">442</a> ;<br>their recognition of caste, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-480">442</a> ; the<br>Jesuits, in 1606, at Madura, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-480">442</a> ; impeded by European hostility against<br>the Order, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-480">442</a> ; and by persecution,<br>especially by Tīpu Sultān, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-480">442</a> ; permitted by Akbar in N. India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-480">442</a> ;<br>Protestant missions—of Lutherans in<br>1705 at Tranquebar, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-480">442</a> ; under Swartz<br>at Tinnevelly, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-481">443</a> ; of Baptists under<br>Carey in 1790 at Serampore, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-481">443</a> ; of<br>English Church, has succeeded to most<br>of the Lutheran missions, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-481">443</a> ; foundation of see of Calcutta in 1814 after<br>labours of Henry Martyn, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-481">443</a> ; Heber,<br>the second Bishop, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-481">443</a> ; of Church of<br>Scotland, under Dr. Duff, one of the<br>pioneers of higher education, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-481">443</a> ; its numbers and regional distribution, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-512">474</a>; proportionately strongest in Kashmīr, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-512">474</a>; explanation of its prevalence in Bengal, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-512">474</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-513">475</a>; its rapid numerical growth, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-513">475</a>; explained by physical and social conditions, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-472">434</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-473">435</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-513">475</a>; comparison of Hindu and Muhammadan birth-rate, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-548">510</a>; death-rate, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-558">520</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-559">521</a>. <em>See also</em> Islām.</p><p class="index-entry">Mullā Ghulām Ahmad, the leader of the Ahmadiyas, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-476">438</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Mundā Languages, recent attention to, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-387">349</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-421">383</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-422">384</a>; often called 'Kolarian,' 382; probably the oldest and aboriginal, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-420">382</a>; relations with remote languages, suggestive of an old common language of India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-420">382</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-424">386</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-425">387</a>; their agglutinative and other characteristics, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-420">382</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-421">383</a>; their home in and around Chotā Nāgpur, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-421">383</a>; a list of them, with number of speakers, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-421">383</a>; Kherwārī, the most important, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-421">383</a>; have neither literature nor proper written character, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-422">384</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Mongoose, found throughout India and Ceylon, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-258">220</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Murrel fish, directly oxygenated from air, can exist in dried mud and recover in wet monsoon, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-319">281</a>. <em>See also</em> Perch, the Climbing.</p><p class="index-entry">'Musk Rat' or Grey Musk Shrew, its smell, haunts, and habits, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-263">225</a>; possibly a commensal of man, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-263">225</a>.</p><h3 class="sec-h2">N.</h3><p class="index-entry">Nāgā group of Tibeto-Burman languages, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-425">387</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Nāgā Hills, most easterly offshoot of the Himālayas, Mongoloid tribe in, divided into <em>khels</em>, exogamous and independent septs, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-347">309</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Nāgās, a semi-divine snake race in the Mahābhārata, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-456">418</a>; name of a prehistoric people, powerful in N. India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-456">418</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Nāgpur, meteorology of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-164">126</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Naipālī, the language of the ruling classes only of Nepāl, a dialect of Rājasthānī, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-406">368</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Narbadā, the river, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-74">36</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-75">37</a>; less prominent in Western Himālayas, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-209">171</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-210">172</a>; in Indus plain, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-216">178</a>; in Bengal proper, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-220">182</a>; in Sundarbans, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-222">184</a>; in Malabar Region, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-225">187</a>; of Nīlgiri Sholas, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-226">188</a>; in Singhbhūm, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-229">191</a>; in Ceylon, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-233">195</a>; in Burma, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-236">198</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-239">201</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-241">203</a>; in Malayan Peninsula, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-243">205</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">‘Oriental,’ different meanings of the term in botany and zoology, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-196">158</a> <em>n.</em></p><p class="index-entry">Orissa, physical aspects of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-64">26</a>; meteorology of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-158">120</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-162">124</a>; ethnology of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-332">294</a>; its language Oriyā, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-414">376</a>; botany of, <em>see</em> Gangetic Plain.</p><p class="index-entry">Oriyā, the language of Orissa and of adjoining parts of Madras and the Central Provinces, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-414">376</a>; isolated and little changed, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-414">376</a>; its considerable literature, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-414">376</a>; the characteristic curves of its writing, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-414">376</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Ormurī, the Iranian language, akin to Pashto, of a small tribe round Kānīgoram, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-393">355</a>; its characteristics, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-393">355</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">‘Outer Band,’ the, languages of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-396">358</a>; represent the earliest immigration of Indo-Aryans, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-396">358</a>; their country and expansion, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-396">358</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-397">359</a>; the present distribution of the Outer languages, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-397">359</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-440">402</a>.</p><h3 class="sec-h2">P.</h3><p class="index-entry">Pachpiriyas, the, of Bengal and United Provinces, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-473">435</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-474">436</a>; worship the five saints, Pānch Pīr, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-473">435</a>; their names and history a compound of Muslim hagiology grafted on Animism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-474">436</a>; their shrine five small clay mounds in a corner of the house, or under the holy village tree, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-474">436</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Pahārī, ‘the language of the Hills,’ an offshoot of Rājasthānī, imposed by conquering Rājputs upon inhabitants of Himālayas from Chamba to Nepāl, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-406">368</a>; modified by (unknown) native speech, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-406">368</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Pālghāt Gap or Pass, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-42">4</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-78">40</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-225">187</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Palī, a secondary Prākrit speech, crystallized by sacred books of Buddhism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-398">360</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Palms, few indigenous, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-198">160</a>; number of species, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-200">162</a>; in Sikkim, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-205">167</a>; in Western Himālayan Region, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-210">172</a>; in Indus plain, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-215">177</a>; in Bengal proper, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-219">181</a>; in Sundarbans, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-220">182</a>; in Malabar Region, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-225">187</a>; in Ceylon, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-233">195</a>; in Burma, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-237">199</a>; in Malayan Peninsula, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-244">206</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Panjābī, the vernacular of Central Punjab, and of the Sikhs, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-407">369</a>; a mixture of Outer Lahndā with Midland, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-407">369</a>; the purest and most arcadian of the Midland speeches, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-407">369</a>; its illegible written character, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-407">369</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Parrots, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-289">251</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Pārsīs; <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-477">439</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-478">440</a>; the Pārsī emigration to India, under pressure of Islām, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-477">439</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-478">440</a>; their settlement at Sanjān, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-478">440</a>; their relations with the Mughal court and Akbar, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-478">440</a>; their spread from Bombay as a commercial centre, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-478">440</a>; their faith submerged in Hinduism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-478">440</a>; its recent emergence, and patriotic revival of the sacred language and books, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-478">440</a>; their factions, Kadīmi and Shenshāi, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-478">440</a>; their dualism and eschatology, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-478">440</a>; their reverence of fire, water, sun, moon, and stars, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-478">440</a>; the exposure of the dead on the Towers of Silence, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-478">440</a>; Bombay and Baroda their principal seats, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-478">440</a>; their numbers, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-478">440</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Passes of the Himālayas, the Shipki group by the Sutlej to Tibet, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-56">18</a>; the Almorā group by the Gogra river to Tibet, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-56">18</a>; the passes from Darjeeling to Southern Tibet and Lhāsa, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-56">18</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Pat desert in Upper Sind and adjacent tracts, hottest area in India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-187">149</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Pathān Frontier, its physical aspects, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-48">10</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-49">11</a>; buffer-land between Afghānistān and British India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-48">10</a>; its chief passes, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-48">10</a>; its language, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-392">354</a>; its tribes, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-393">355</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Pathāns or speakers of the Pashto language, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-47">9</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-392">354</a>; Afghān type of tribe among, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-347">309</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Peat, of Nepāl, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-139">101</a>; of Nīlgiri and Anaimalai Hills, 189 and <em>n.</em>; rarity of bog-land in the Himālayas, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-197">159</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Penang Islet, botany of, the vast number of its species, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-245">207</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Peninsula, the, geology of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-88">50</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-89">51</a>; meteorology of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-150">112</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-152">114</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-161">123</a>; botany of, compared with Burmese, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-223">185</a>; zoology of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-252">214</a>. <em>See also</em> Deccan, Malabar, <em>and</em> Coromandel.</p><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-596"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="596" data-folio="558" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/imperialgazette01hunt/page/n595_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 558">[p. 558]</button></div><p class="index-entry">Perch, the Climbing, can exist without<br>water, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-319">281</a>. <em>See also</em> Murrel.</p><p class="index-entry">Peshāwar valley, the, its physical aspects,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-66">28</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-67">29</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Phthisis, high death-rate from, in Bombay, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-558">520</a>; due to overcrowding and a<br>low standard of domestic hygiene, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-542">504</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-558">520</a>; death-rate from, in European<br>army, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-564">526</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-565">527</a>; in Native army, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-567">529</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-568">530</a>; in jails, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-568">530</a>; comparison of<br>mortality from, between European and<br>Native troops and prisoners, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-571">533</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Physical aspects of India, chap. <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/01-frontmatter/#page-7">i</a>, pp.<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-39">1</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-87">49</a>; their extreme variety, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-39">1</a>; geological evolution, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-39">1</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-42">4</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-76">38</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-77">39</a>; origin<br>of name of India and modern extension of term, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-42">4</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-43">5</a>;
