the<br>Kistna, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-83">45</a>; the Cauvery, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-83">45</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-84">46</a>; their<br>deltas, the granaries of the Peninsula,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-80">42</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-83">45</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-84">46</a>; the physical and moral<br>characteristics of South-Eastern India,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-84">46</a>; Tinnevelly and Madura, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-84">46</a>; Travancore, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-84">46</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-85">47</a>; Ceylon, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-85">47</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-87">49</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Physical characters, the most trustworthy<br>data of ethnology, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-321">283</a>; indefinite or<br>secondary, of skin, eyes, hair, face, and<br>features, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-321">283</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-322">284</a>; definite or anthropometric, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-322">284</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-330">292</a>; consequent division<br>of people of Indian Empire into seven<br>main physical types, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-330">292</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-334">296</a>; limitations of the type scheme, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-335">297</a>; its<br>general acceptance in England and the<br>Continent, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-324">286</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Physiography of India, main outlines in<br>connexion with meteorology, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-145">107</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-146">108</a>; direct origin of lower air movements not from Central Asia, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-146">108</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Pigeons and Doves, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-293">255</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Pigs, wild, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-275">237</a>; distribution, haunts, and<br>habits, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-275">237</a>; the Wild Boar, the Pigmy<br>Hog, ‘pig-sticking,’ 237.</p><p class="index-entry">Pinjrapols, animal hospitals in which<br>even vermin are protected and fed,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-452">414</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Pisācha languages and tribes, now found<br>only between the Hindu Kush and the</p><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-597"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="597" data-folio="559" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/imperialgazette01hunt/page/n596_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 559">[p. 559]</button></div><p class="index-entry">Punjab, once more widely distributed,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-393">355</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-394">356</a>; between Eranian and Indo-Aryan; their archaic character, their<br>dialects, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-394">356</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Plague, mortality from, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-563">525</a>. <em>See</em> Vol.<br>IV, chap. <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/03-table-of-contents/#page-20">xiv</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Plovers, Snipes, and Woodcocks, 260–<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-300">262</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Polyandry, of Draupadī, the wife of the<br>five Pāṇḍava brethren in the Mahābhārata, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-457">419</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-462">424</a>; of Central and<br>Western Punjab and of modern Jāts and<br>Santāls, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-410">372</a>; two types of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-521">483</a>; matriarchal, of simultaneous alliance with two<br>or more men not necessarily related,<br>with succession through the female,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-521">483</a>; once, not now, prevalent on the<br>Malabar coast, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-521">483</a>; fraternal, of<br>marriage with several brothers, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-521">483</a>;<br>still prevalent along Himālayas from<br>Kashmīr to Assam, and among the<br>Todas, and elsewhere, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-462">424</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-521">483</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Polygamy, rare in India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-520">482</a>; excess of<br>wives per thousand, thirty-one among<br>Animists, twenty-one among Muhammadans, eight among Hindus, seven<br>among Buddhists, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-520">482</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-521">483</a>; among<br>Christians an excess of husbands, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-521">483</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Pomfrets, the ‘silver,’ the ‘gray,’ the<br>white, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-318">280</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Poona, dry and liable to droughts, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-180">142</a>;<br>Desi, a dialect of Marāthī, the language<br>of the District, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-412">374</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Population, chap. <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/02-general-preface/#page-15">ix</a>, pp. <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-485">447</a>–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-526">488</a>; general<br>characteristics of Indian as compared<br>with Western peoples, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-485">447</a>–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-487">449</a>; area<br>and population of India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-487">449</a>; diagram<br>of relative area and population of the<br>various Provinces and chief Native<br>States, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-488">450</a>; density of population,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-489">451</a>; in Bengal, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-490">452</a>; growth of population<br>since 1867, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-497">459</a>; conditions affecting<br>growth of population, 1891–1901,<br>capacity of India to support a greater<br>population, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-499">461</a>; one God, but of Brāhmans only,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-462">424</a>.</p><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-599"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="599" data-folio="561" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/imperialgazette01hunt/page/n598_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 561">[p. 561]</button></div><p class="index-entry">Purāna group of rocks, its two divisions<br>and distribution, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-99">61</a>–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-102">64</a>; in Burma, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-100">62</a>;<br>possibly in the Himālayas, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-101">63</a>.</p><h3 class="sec-h2">Q</h3><p class="index-entry">Quetta, physical aspects of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-45">7</a>; easy<br>routes practicable from it to the Persian<br>frontier, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-46">8</a>.</p><h3 class="sec-h2">R</h3><p class="index-entry">Rādhāswāmīs, a modern reformed sect of<br>Vaishnavism in the United Provinces,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-465">427</a>; founded by Shiu Dayāl Singh<br>(A.D. 1818–78), a Khattrī of Agra, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-465">427</a>;<br>the three spheres of the universe, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-465">427</a>;<br>the second presided over by the God<br>of the Bible, of the Vedantists, and of the<br>Muhammadan saints, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-466">428</a>; its doctrine<br>of progressive transmigration to the<br>presence of the Supreme, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-466">428</a>; has no<br>priests and no temples, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-466">428</a>; reverence<br>paid to its spiritual head, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-466">428</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Rainfall, normal and abnormal, annual<br>measurements of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-142">104</a>; chart of normal rainfall in the<br>plains, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-169">131</a>; contraction of rainfall<br>during retreating south-west monsoon,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-169">131</a>; special remarks<br>on rainfall in India and droughts,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-176">138</a>–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-184">146</a>; cold-weather rainfall, due to<br>storms, its amount, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-178">140</a>; hot-weather<br>rainfall, its amount, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-178">140</a>; greatest in<br>Assam, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-179">141</a>; generally of little value,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-179">141</a>; rainfall of south-west monsoon,<br>nearly 90 per cent. of whole due<br>to oceanic currents from Arabian Sea<br>and Bay of Bengal, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-179">141</a>; distribution,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-180">142</a>; cyclonic storms of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-181">143</a>; excessive<br>downpours of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-181">143</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-182">144</a>; its variability,<br>with tables, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-182">144</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-183">145</a>; deficient rainfall and droughts, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-183">145</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-184">146</a>, <em>see also</em><br>Droughts; rainfall data of twenty-one<br>meteorological divisions of India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-191">153</a>;<br>variation of density of population with rainfall, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-489">451</a>; southern, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-72">34</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-73">35</a>;<br>the country of the ancient Rājput dynasties and cities, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-72">34</a>; Mārwārī, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-405">367</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-406">368</a>; its offshoots,<br>Pahārī, Naipālī, Gujarī, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-406">368</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Rājputāna Sea of Palaeozoic times, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-39">1</a>;<br>edged by the Arāvallis, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-39">1</a>; now the<br>Thar or great desert of India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-71">33</a>; existed during Upper Jurassic times, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-114">76</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Rājputs, typical representatives of Indo-Aryan family, physical characters of,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-331">293</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-337">299</a>; intermarriage with Jāts, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-338">300</a>;<br>manufacture of fictitious, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-350">312</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-358">320</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-359">321</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-365">327</a>; abstain from widow remarriage,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-360">322</a>; next in rank to Brāhmans, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-364">326</a>;<br>their number, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-365">327</a>; the dispersion of<br>the Rājput powers and clans by the<br>Muslim conquest, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-471">433</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Rām Mohan Roy (1774–1833), founder<br>of the Brahmo Samāj, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-467">429</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Rāma, an incarnation of Vishnu, the god<br>of the orthodox Brāhman, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-462">424</a>; possibly<br>once a local Rājput hero of Kosala,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-462">424</a>; connexion of his cult with Buddhism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-462">424</a>; preserves the kindliness of<br>Buddhism and its tenderness for animal<br>life, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-462">424</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Rāmāyana, the, an epic, a collection of<br>popular eastern legends, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-457">419</a>; its probable date, 500–200 B. C., <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-456">418</a>; Rāma<br>and Sītā, models of virtue, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-457">419</a>; still<br>the Vaishnava Bible in its Eastern<br>Hindī rendering by Tulsi Dās, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-457">419</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Rangoon, meteorology of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-164">126</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Rann of Cutch, the, its physical aspects<br>and recent emergence, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-76">38</a>; geology<br>of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-113">75</a>; partial submersion by earthquake of 1819, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-137">99</a>; meteorology of,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-161">123</a>; botany of, <em>see</em> Indus Plain.</p><p class="index-entry">Rats and Mice, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-265">227</a>–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-267">229</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Rāvi, the, an affluent of the Indus, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-70">32</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Rāwalpindi, temperatures at, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-188">150</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-190">152</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-192">154</a>; largely based<br>upon primitive Animism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-468">430</a>–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-470">432</a>.</p><div class="page-rail-marker" id="page-600"><button type="button" class="folio-trigger" data-page="600" data-folio="562" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/imperialgazette01hunt/page/n599_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect scan for page 562">[p. 562]</button></div><p class="index-entry">Religions, ch. <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/02-general-preface/#page-14">viii</a>, pp. <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-440">402</a>–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-484">446</a>. Vedic period (<em>c.</em> 1500–200 <span class="sc">b. c.</span>), <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-440">402</a>; the Aryan religion, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-440">402</a>; the Vedas, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-440">402</a>; Vedic theology, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-441">403</a>; the gods departmental, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-442">404</a>; beginnings of Pantheism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-442">404</a>. The Brāhmaṇa period (<em>c.</em> 800–500 <span class="sc">b. c.</span>), <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-442">404</a>; the supremacy of the priesthood, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-442">404</a>; theology and worship in the Brāhmaṇas, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-443">405</a>; life after death, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-443">405</a>; human sacrifice, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-443">405</a>; theology in the Upanishads, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-444">406</a>; the anti-Brāhmanical reaction, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-444">406</a>; Gautama, the Buddha (<em>c.</em> 596–508 <span class="sc">b. c.</span>), <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-445">407</a>. Buddhism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-446">408</a>–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-451">413</a>; its origin, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-446">408</a>; its relation to caste, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-446">408</a>; its ethics, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-447">409</a>; its theology and psychology, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-447">409</a>; their struggles with Buddhism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-459">421</a>; the repulsive asceticism of some, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-459">421</a>; the Smārtas and the Lingāyats, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-459">421</a>–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-461">423</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Sāktas, the third great sect of Hinduism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-465">427</a>; worships the active female principle, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-465">427</a>; its impure associations and abominable rites, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-465">427</a>; opposed by the Vaishnava reformers, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-465">427</a>; its recent spread in Upper India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-465">427</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Salt Range of the Punjab, geology of, the oldest fossiliferous strata in India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-102">64</a>; <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-601" data-page="601" data-folio="563" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/imperialgazette01hunt/page/n600_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 563">[p. 563]</button> division of its Cambrian strata, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-102">64</a>;<br>gaps in its geological record, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-103">65</a>; Permian boulder-bed, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-108">70</a>; Productus shales<br>and limestones, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-109">71</a>; passage to Triassic<br>strata, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-110">72</a>; the Ceratite formations, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-111">73</a>;<br>Jurassic strata, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-114">76</a>; Nummulitic rocks,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-130">92</a>; botany of Rāwalpindi, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-217">179</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Samāj, modern theistic sects, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-467">429</a>; the<br>Brahmo Samāj, founded by Rām Mohan<br>Roy (1774-1883), <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-467">429</a>; a form of<br>Unitarianism of European type, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-467">429</a>;<br>its three sections, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-467">429</a>: the Adi Samāj,<br>oldest and most conservative, adheres<br>to rules and rites of Hinduism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-467">429</a>;<br>the Nabibidhan Samāj, founded by<br>Keshub Chunder Sen, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-467">429</a>; eclectic<br>from Hinduism, Christianity, Buddhism, and Islām, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-467">429</a>; the Sadhāran<br>Brahmo Samāj, the most advanced,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-467">429</a>; rejects caste, seclusion of women,<br>and Hinduism generally, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-467">429</a>; attracts<br>Hindus of English education, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-467">429</a>;<br>the Arya Samāj, founded by Dayānand Saraswatī (1827-53), <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-467">429</a>; the<br>Vedas their only scripture, pure monotheists, devoted to social improvement,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-467">429</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-468">430</a>; of influence in NW. India,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-467">429</a>; their number, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-511">473</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-512">474</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Sand-grouse, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-293">255</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Sankarāchārya, one of the two great<br>missionaries of Sivaism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-459">421</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-460">422</a>;<br>founded monasteries from Kumaun to<br>Mysore, and the sect of Smārtas, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-459">421</a>;<br>a main cause of the downfall of Buddhism in N. India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-459">421</a>; deified by<br>Saivas as incarnation of Siva, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-459">421</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Sanskrit, original dialect of the 'Midland,' the true home of the Indo-Aryans, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-395">357</a>; its early literary culture,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-395">357</a>; a polished form of an archaic<br>tongue, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-395">357</a>; no longer a vernacular in<br>300 <span class="sc">B.C.</span>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-395">357</a>; its evolution and relation<br>to Prākrit, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-395">357</a>; analogy of Latin and<br>Romance languages, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-396">358</a>; no modern<br>Indian language directly derived from<br>Sanskrit, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-396">358</a>; its prestige and influence upon modern vernaculars, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-400">362</a>;<br><em>tatsamas</em>, <em>tadbhavas</em>, and <em>desyas</em>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-401">363</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Santāls, a typical Dravidian tribe of<br>Chotā Nāgpur, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-334">296</a>; their polyandry,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-410">372</a>; their language Santālī, of the<br>Mundā family, much studied lately,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-421">383</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Satnāmis, a modern reformed Vaishnava sect, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-466">428</a>; founded by an Oudh<br>Rājput, Jagjīvandās, early in seventeenth century, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-466">428</a>; and spread among<br>his own caste by the Chamār Ghāsidās (1820-30 <span class="sc">A. D.</span>), <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-466">428</a>; its seven<br>principles, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-466">428</a>; its purity and morality<br>debased, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-466">428</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Sātpurā Hills, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-74">36</a>; break near Khandwā,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-74">36</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-75">37</a>; geology of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-121">83</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Saurasenī, a Prākrit of the Midland, close<br>to great kingdom of Kanauj, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-399">361</a>; the<br>prose language of the Indian dramas,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-399">361</a>; its vocabulary that of Sanskrit, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-400">362</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Scottish Church, its missions, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-481">443</a>; Dr.<br>Duff, one of the pioneers of higher<br>education, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-481">443</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Scythians, invasions by, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-343">305</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-346">308</a>. <em>See</em><br>Invasions and immigrations, Scytho-Dravidian.</p><p class="index-entry">Scytho-Dravidian Race or Type, the,<br>its distribution and physical characteristics, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-331">293</a>; evidence from China, coins,<br>and Indian tradition of successive invasions of India by Scythians from the<br>west, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-343">305</a>; historical record of Scythian invasions, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-343">305</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-344">306</a>; possible<br>course of Scythians from Western<br>Punjab southwards to become, with<br>indigenous Dravidians, ancestors of<br>the Marāthās, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-344">306</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-346">308</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Seasons, Cold, Dry, Hot, Wet; <em>see</em> Cold,<br>Dry, Hot, Wet Seasons.</p><p class="index-entry">Sectarianism in modern Hinduism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-468">430</a>;<br>theoretically prominent, practically<br>softened or obliterated by eclectic spirit<br>of the system, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-468">430</a>; the god of one<br>sect or individual may be in a less<br>degree a god of other sects and individuals, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-468">430</a>; the sanctity of holy<br>places common to many creeds, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-468">430</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Senart, M., <em>Les Castes dans l'Inde</em>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-375">337</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-377">339</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-380">342</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Sex, statistics of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-517">479</a>; normal excess in<br>India of male over female births, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-518">480</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-548">510</a>; 963 females living to every 1,000<br>males, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-517">479</a>; proportion of males to<br>females in different parts, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-517">479</a>; majority of males in the western half, of<br>females in the eastern, except in Bengal<br>proper and Assam, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-517">479</a>; recent increase<br>of females, as the stronger sex, especially in Central Provinces, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-517">479</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-518">480</a>;<br>explanations of deficiency of females,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-518">480</a>; not female infanticide, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-518">480</a>;<br>neglect, female risks, and possibly race,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-518">480</a>; male and female death-rates, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-554">516</a>;<br>highest female mortality from ten to<br>thirty-four years, the years of procreation and its risks, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-554">516</a>; the huge<br>loss of life in childbed, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-554">516</a>; the value<br>of the Dufferin Association, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-554">516</a>; occupations common to men and women,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-526">488</a>; peculiar to women, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-526">488</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Shad, <em>hilsa</em> of Bengal, 'Sable fish' of<br>S. India, 'Palla' of the Indus, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-315">277</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Shāgirdpeshas of Bengal, a true caste,<br>formed by crossing, and adopting endogamy, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-355">317</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Shan, a Tai language, allied to Siamese,<br>spoken in Upper Burma, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-426">388</a>; its voluminous literature, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-427">389</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Sharks, abundant in Indian seas, one high <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-602" data-page="602" data-folio="564" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/imperialgazette01hunt/page/n601_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 564">[p. 564]</button> up large rivers, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-313">275</a>; the Hammer-headed shark, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-313">275</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Sheep (wild), found in Himālayas and<br>hills of Punjab and Sind, the Great<br>Tibetan (<em>Ovis hodgsoni</em>), the Great<br>Pamir (<em>Ovis poli</em>), <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-271">233</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Shiahs, the, a main sect of Islām, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-474">436</a>;<br>reject the first three Imāms as usurpers,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-474">436</a>; observe the feast of Muharram in<br>memory of martyrdom of Alī and his<br>two sons, the grandsons of the Prophet,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-474">436</a>; their parade of the <em>taziās</em>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-474">436</a>;<br>form a large majority in Persia and<br>Afghānistān, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-474">436</a>; Lucknow and Hyderābād their chief seats in India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-474">436</a>.<br><em>See</em> Sunnis.</p><p class="index-entry">Sholāpur, meteorology of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-164">126</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-180">142</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Sholas, indigenous forests of Nīlgiris,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-78">40</a>; botany of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-226">188</a>; of Anaimalais and<br>Palnis, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-226">188</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Sibsāgar, meteorology of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-164">126</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Sikhism, a religious reform ending in a<br>political organization, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-464">426</a>; founded in<br>the Punjab by Guru Nānak (1469–<br>1538 <span class="sc">A.D.</span>), his formula, the Unity of<br>God and the Brotherhood of Man, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-465">427</a>;<br>inspired by Kabīr, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-465">427</a>; the five marks<br>or <em>ka</em>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-465">427</a>; the Granth or Sikh Bible,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-465">427</a>; its inspiring influence, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-331">293</a>;<br>tendency to revert to Brāhmanism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-565">527</a>;<br>the number of its adherents, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-511">473</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Sikkim, the most humid region of Himālayas, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-204">166</a>; tropical<br>zone, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-205">167</a>; temperate zone, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-206">168</a>; alpine<br>zone, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-207">169</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Silurian (Gothlandian) beds, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-104">66</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Simla, on the divide between the Sutlej<br>and the Jumna, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-57">19</a>; its mean temperature, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-193">155</a>; <em>Flora Simlensis</em>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-210">172</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Sind, Tertiary marine rocks of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-130">92</a>; the coarser<br>elements of his worship possibly Dravidian, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-458">420</a>; the Destroyer and Reproducer, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-458">420</a>; the Yogi, the sage, the<br>Dionysos, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-457">419</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-458">420</a>; as Visvesvara,<br>‘Lord of the Universe,’ his <em>lingam</em><br>the chief object of worship at Benares,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-459">421</a>; has shrines in most villages, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-459">421</a>;<br>his worship cheap and plain, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-459">421</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Siwālik (geological) system, the, alluvial<br>pliocene deposits rich in vertebrate<br>fossils, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-134">96</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-135">97</a>; age of, determined by Irrawaddy<br>system, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-135">97</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Siwālik Hills, physical aspects of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-55">17</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Small-pox, the times of its prevalence,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-562">524</a>; recent decrease in mortality from,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-562">524</a>; table of mortality from, and of<br>successful vaccination during 1871–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-118">80</a>,<br>1881–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-128">90</a>, 1891–1900, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-563">525</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Smārtas, the, a Saiva sect of Brāhmans<br>of the S. Deccan, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-459">421</a>; founded by<br>Sankarāchārya, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-459">421</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-460">422</a>; their teaching representative of the highest form<br>of Brāhmanic pantheism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-460">422</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Snakes, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-307">269</a>–<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-310">272</a>. <em>See</em> Zoology.</p><p class="index-entry">Snakes, Colubridae, divided according to<br>teeth into (<em>a</em>) harmless, (<em>b</em>) slightly<br>poisonous, (<em>c</em>) most poisonous, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-308">270</a>;<br>(<em>a</em>) the ‘carpet snake,’ the <em>dhāman</em><br>or Rat snake, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-308">270</a>; (<em>b</em>) water snakes,<br>the ‘whip snake,’ 271; (<em>c</em>) <em>Hydrophiinae</em>, sea snakes, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-309">271</a>; <em>Elapinae</em>;<br>the Cobras; <em>Naia tripudians</em>, the<br>Great Cobra (<em>Hamadryas</em> or <em>Ophiophagus</em>), the <em>karait</em>, the <em>rāj-sāmp</em> or<br>King-snake, the black Cobra; their<br>marks and distribution, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-309">271</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-310">272</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Soma, the moon-plant, Vedic worship of,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-441">403</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Southern India, physical aspects of, 37–<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-85">47</a>; rigid classification of castes in,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-364">326</a>; elaboration of idea of ceremonial<br>pollution, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-364">326</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Specific fevers, high mortality from, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-561">523</a>;<br>their communicability not the only<br>factor in their spread and fatality, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-561">523</a>;<br>contributory causes, deficient resistance, due to poverty, chronic or acute,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-561">523</a>; insanitary habits and surroundings, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-561">523</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-562">524</a>; tables of mortality<br>from, in all India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-559">521</a>; among European troops, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-564">526</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-565">527</a>; in Native army,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-567">529</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-568">530</a>; in jails, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-568">530</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-569">531</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Squirrels, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-264">226</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-265">227</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Stature, Topinard’s classification of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-330">292</a>;<br>as index of race more significant in<br>India than in Europe, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-330">292</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Storks, the Adjutant, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-302">264</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Storms and cyclones, of the cold-weather<br>period, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-150">112</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-151">113</a>; of dust, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-155">117</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Subsistence, means of, unequal to population, in United Provinces, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-504">466</a>, in Magadha, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-413">375</a>; equal to present growth of<br>population in India generally, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-499">461</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-500">462</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Sūdras, the, the fourth and lowest of the <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-603" data-page="603" data-folio="565" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/imperialgazette01hunt/page/n602_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 565">[p. 565]</button> original castes or groups, the class of artisans and servants, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-370">332</a>; a late invention or formation, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-372">334</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-373">335</a>; their hopeless state during the period of the Brāhmanas, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-445">407</a>; the term now out of favour, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-363">325</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Sūfiism, a minor sect of Islām, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-475">437</a>; a strange combination of Aryan pantheism with Semitic monotheism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-475">437</a>; the soul an emanation from God, ever seeking to rejoin its source, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-475">437</a>; ecstasy as a means of approach, absorption the end, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-475">437</a>; accepted by leading Sunnis, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-475">437</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Sundarbans, botany of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-220">182</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-222">184</a>. <em>See also</em> Ganges, Gangetic Plain, Deltas.</p><p class="index-entry">Sunnis, the, or Traditionalists, a main sect of Islām, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-474">436</a>; unlike the Shiahs, accept the Sunnat as concurrent with or supplementary to the Korān, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-474">436</a>; form the vast majority in Turkey and India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-474">436</a>. <em>See</em> Shiahs.</p><p class="index-entry">Sutlej, the, chief affluent of the Indus, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-69">31</a>; its rise, course, length, and scenery, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-69">31</a>; its affluent, the Beās, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-70">32</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Svetāmbara, or 'white-clothed' sect of Jains in N. and W. India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-452">414</a>; the only possessors of the older sacred books, the Angas and Pūrvas, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-452">414</a>; their schism from the Digambara, 'those clothed with the sky' of the south, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-452">414</a>; who worship naked idols and revere their Gurus and deny the salvation of women, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-455">417</a>; the Dhondiyas, who worship their Gurus, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-455">417</a>.</p><h3 class="sec-h2">T.</h3><p class="index-entry">Tables and Classifications other than statistical. Geological: the arrangement and chief divisions of the four great groups of Indian rocks, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-93">55</a>; the strata of the fossiliferous Cretaceous rocks of Trichinopoly, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-116">78</a>; Tertiary formations in Burma, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-133">95</a>. Botanical: the nine Regions, subdivided into sixty-four Provinces, of Indian Flora, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-201">163</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-203">165</a>. Zoological: the three zoological areas of India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-252">214</a>. Linguistic: a classified list of modern Indo-Aryan vernaculars, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-402">364</a>; classified list of modern Dravidian vernaculars, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-417">379</a>; a list of the Mundā languages, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-421">383</a>; a classified list of Indo-Chinese languages of India and Nepāl, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-428">390</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-432">394</a>; a list of minor languages spoken in India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-432">394</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Tamil, or Arava, the oldest, richest, and most organized of Dravidian tongues, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-418">380</a>; the language of all S. India to Mysore and the Ghāts on the west, and as far north as Madras city, and of N. Ceylon, and of domestic servants, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-418">380</a>; has a copious literature and its own alphabet, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-418">380</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Tāpti, river of Western India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-74">36</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">'Tarai,' different meanings of term in West Himālayan region and in Sikkim, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-205">167</a>; usual or correct meaning, the swampy lowlands at the foot of the Lower Himālayas and below the belt of forest, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/07-chapter-iv-botany/#page-205">167</a>, <a class="index-folio-link"
