characteristics of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-461">423</a>; its<br>foundations in the Purāna, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-462">424</a>; its <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-605" data-page="605" data-folio="567" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/imperialgazette01hunt/page/n604_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 567">[p. 567]</button> gods, Krishna and Rāmā, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-461">423</a>; originally the religion of Brāhmans only,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-462">424</a>; its reformers and popularizers,<br>Rāmānuja, Kabīr, Rāmānand, Chaitanya, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-463">425</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-464">426</a>; its sects and developments, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-464">426</a>; erotic, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-464">426</a>; Sikhism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-464">426</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-465">427</a>; modern reform, the Rādhāswāmīs,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-465">427</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-466">428</a>; the Satnāmis, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-466">428</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Vaiṣya, one of the four original castes or<br>groups, the trading and agricultural<br>classes, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-370">332</a>; during the period of the<br>Brāhmanas regarded as mere supporters of the expenses of the sacrificial<br>system, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-445">407</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Vallabhāchāryas, a sect of erotic Vaishnavism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-464">426</a>; chiefly in W. India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-464">426</a>;<br>their Gosain or leader regarded as a<br>divinity, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-464">426</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Vardhamāna (599-527 B.C.), the founder<br>of Jainism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-452">414</a>; of the same class,<br>time, and country as Gautama, gained<br>the name of Mahāvīra, ‘the great hero,’<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-452">414</a>; assumed that of Jina, ‘the<br>victorious,’ 414.</p><p class="index-entry">Variations in population, <em>see</em> Census<br>Table II, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-527">489</a>; conditions and causes<br>of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-498">460</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-499">461</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Vedas, the, a collection of Hymns,<br>generally ritualistic, mainly the work<br>of priests, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-440">402</a>; composed at different<br>stages of Aryan immigration, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-440">402</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-441">403</a>;<br>Vedic theology, its departmental gods<br>of sky, air, and earth, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-441">403</a>; beginnings<br>of anthropomorphism, pantheism, and<br>of the idea of a Father-God, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-441">403</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-442">404</a>;<br>the Ātman or all-pervading spirit, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-442">404</a>;<br>the birth in ‘Midland’ of Brāhmanism,<br>the supremacy of ritual, sacrifice, and<br>priesthood, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-442">404</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-443">405</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Vernacular languages of India, five<br>families, Aryan, Dravidian, Mundā,<br>Mon-Khmer, and Tibeto-Chinese, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-389">351</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Vernaculars, modern Indo-Aryan, their<br>origin, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-397">359</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-400">362</a>; their descent from<br>Apabhramsas, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-400">362</a>; their borrowings,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-401">363</a>; a list of vernaculars and numbers<br>of speakers, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-402">364</a>; their distribution,<br>history, character, and literature, 364-<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/10-chapter-vii-languages/#page-416">378</a>. <em>See also</em> Languages.</p><p class="index-entry">Vertebrates, number of genera and<br>species, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-251">213</a>; distribution of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-251">213</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-252">214</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Villages, inhabited by nine-tenths of the<br>population, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-493">455</a>; 728,605 in number,<br>with an average population of 364,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-493">455</a>; the term very various in its<br>meaning, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/12-chapter-ix-population/#page-494">456</a>; comparison of urban<br>and rural mortality, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-556">518</a>. <em>See</em> Census<br>Tables I, IV.</p><p class="index-entry">Vindhyan (geological) system, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-100">62</a>;<br>mainly sandstone with shale and<br>limestone, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-100">62</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Vindhyas, range of mountains, physical<br>aspects of, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-73">35</a>; dividing basins of</p><p class="index-entry">Ganges and Mahānadī, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-73">35</a>; their<br>geology, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-100">62</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Vipers: Russell’s Viper or Chain-viper<br>and Cobra monil of S. India, or <em>tic-polonga</em> of Ceylon; <em>Echis carinata</em> or<br>the Kappa; Pit Vipers, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-310">272</a>. For other<br>poisonous snakes, <em>see</em> Snakes.</p><p class="index-entry">Vishnu, the second person of the Hindu<br>Triad, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-457">419</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-458">420</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-461">423</a>; in the Rig-Veda<br>and the Mahābhārata, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-457">419</a>; compared<br>with Siva, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-458">420</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-461">423</a>; characteristics<br>of Vaishnavism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-461">423</a>; his Avatāras or<br>incarnations in animal or human shape,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-461">423</a>; his manifold forms, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-461">423</a>; his<br>incarnations as Krishna and Rāma, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-461">423</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Volcanoes, action of, at close of Cretaceous period, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-40">2</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/04-chapter-i-physical-aspects/#page-41">3</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-125">87</a>; recent, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-136">98</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Vrātya, the twice-born who have<br>neglected duties and rites, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/09-chapter-vi-ethnology-and-caste/#page-372">334</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Vultures, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-290">252</a>.</p><h3 class="sec-h2">W.</h3><p class="index-entry">Wahābis, a minor sect of Islām, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-474">436</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-475">437</a>; founded by Ibn Abdul Wahāb,<br>in Arabia, early in eighteenth<br>century, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-474">436</a>; puritanical, fanatical,<br>political, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-474">436</a>; partial agreement<br>with Sunnis, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-475">437</a>; protest against<br>reverence to Imāms and saints, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-476">438</a>;<br>introduced into India by Sayid Ahmad<br>Shāh, 1826; a constant trouble on<br>NW. frontier, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-476">438</a>; their dangerous<br>teaching of religious war against the<br>rulers of India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-476">438</a>; some recent decay<br>of fanaticism, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-476">438</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Water-supply, its dependence upon the<br>character of the monsoon, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-540">502</a>; its<br>collection in surface tanks and wells, in<br>rivers, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-541">503</a>; its liability to pollution,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-541">503</a>; excess, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-562">524</a>; and deficiency, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-541">503</a>;<br>the diseases consequent thereon, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-541">503</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-542">504</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-561">523</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-562">524</a>; provision of pure water<br>supplies, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-541">503</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-557">519</a>; defective drainage,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-541">503</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/13-chapter-x-public-health-and-vital-statistics/#page-561">523</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Weaver Birds, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-282">244</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Wet season, from June to September,<br>changes of pressure, with a graphic,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-157">119</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-158">120</a>; of air movement, with a<br>graphic, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-158">120</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-159">121</a>; currents in Arabian<br>Sea, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-158">120</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-160">122</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-161">123</a>; in Bay of Bengal,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-160">122</a>; date of establishment of wet<br>season in different parts of India, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-162">124</a>;<br>marked tendency to uniformity of<br>temperature, cloud, and humidity conditions, with table, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-164">126</a>; mean rainfall,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/06-chapter-iii-meteorology/#page-178">140</a>. <em>See also</em> Monsoon, Rainfall,<br>Temperature.</p><p class="index-entry">Whales, no Right Whale (Balaena) in<br>Indian waters, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-276">238</a>; Fin Whales,<br>Sperm Whales, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-276">238</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">‘Whiting’ of Madras and Calcutta, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-318">280</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Winds. <em>See</em> Monsoon, Storms.</p><p class="index-entry">Wolves, not found in Ceylon or Burma, <button type="button" class="folio-trigger folio-turn" id="page-606" data-page="606" data-folio="568" data-scan="https://archive.org/download/imperialgazette01hunt/page/n605_w1400.jpg" aria-label="Inspect original scan for page 568">[p. 568]</button> dangerous to children, superstition<br>against killing, tales of children reared<br>by, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-259">221</a>.</p><h3 class="sec-h2">X.</h3><p class="index-entry">Xavier, Francis, Saint, his labours in S.<br>Madras and Malabar coast, death in<br>China, burial at Goa, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-480">442</a>.</p><h3 class="sec-h2">· Y.</h3><p class="index-entry">Yaks, wild and tame, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-269">231</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-270">232</a>; interbreed with domestic cattle, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-270">232</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Yenangyaung (geological) series, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-132">94</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/05-chapter-ii-geology/#page-137">99</a>.</p><h3 class="sec-h2">Z.</h3><p class="index-entry">Zoology (vertebrate) of British India,<br>chap. iv, pp. <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-251">213</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-320">282</a>. anseres,<br>swans, geese, and ducks, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-303">265</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-304">266</a>;<br>pygopodes or grebes, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-304">266</a>.<br>III. crocodiles,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-304">266</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-305">267</a>; chelonia, tortoises and<br>turtles, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-305">267</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-306">268</a>; squamata, lizards,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-306">268</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-307">269</a>; snakes, all the known<br>families inhabit India, and India alone,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-307">269</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-310">272</a>; pythons and boas, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-307">269</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-308">270</a>;<br>colubridae, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-308">270</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-309">271</a>; the cobras, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-309">271</a>,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-310">272</a>; the vipers, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-310">272</a>.<br>IV. Batrachians, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-310">272</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-312">274</a>; ecaudata,<br>or frogs and toads, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-311">273</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-312">274</a>; caudata, or newts and salamanders, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-312">274</a>;<br>apoda, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-312">274</a>.<br>V. Fishes, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-312">274</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-320">282</a>; chondropterygii,<br>sharks, saw-fishes, dog-fishes, rays, and<br>skates, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-313">275</a>; teleostei, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-314">276</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-320">282</a>; including (with others) eels, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-314">276</a>; cat-fishes,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-314">276</a>; carps, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-314">276</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-315">277</a>; herrings, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-315">277</a>;<br>‘Bombay duck,’ 278; perch, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-316">278</a>;<br>mullets, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-316">278</a>, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-317">279</a>; the mango-fish,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-317">279</a>; sword-fishes, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-317">279</a>; horse-mackerels, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-317">279</a>; the pomfrets, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-318">280</a>; mackerel and tunny, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-318">280</a>; Madras and<br>Calcutta ‘whiting,’ 280; ‘mud-skippers’ and spiny eels, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-318">280</a>; murrel,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-318">280</a>; climbing perch, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-319">281</a>; flat fishes,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-320">282</a>; pipe-fishes and sea-horses, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-320">282</a>;<br>plectognath, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-320">282</a>; amphioxus or lancelet, <a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/08-chapter-v-zoology/#page-320">282</a>.</p><p class="index-entry">Zoroastrianism, or the Pārsī religion,<br><a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-477">439</a>-<a class="index-folio-link" href="/books/imperial-gazetteer-of-india-vol-01-descriptive/chapters/11-chapter-viii-religions/#page-479">441</a>.</p><div class="prose-unit"><p class="imprint-line">Oxford: Printed at the Clarendon Press by <span class="sc">Horace Hart, M.A.</span></p></div>
